OccVLA).\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport torch\\nimport torch.nn as nn\\nimport torch.nn.functional as F\\n\\n\\nclass BEVOccupancyHead(nn.Module):\\n    def __init__(self, in_dim: int, out_resolution: int = 64, num_classes: int = 1,\\n                 hidden_dim: int = 256, num_patches_per_image: int = 256):\\n        super().__init__()\\n        self.num_patches_per_image = num_patches_per_image\\n        self.spatial_h = self.spatial_w = int(num_patches_per_image ** 0.5)  # 16\\n        self.out_resolution = out_resolution\\n        self.num_classes = num_classes\\n\\n        self.project = nn.Linear(in_dim, hidden_dim)\\n        self.decoder = nn.Sequential(\\n            nn.ConvTranspose2d(hidden_dim, 128, kernel_size=4, stride=2, padding=1),  # 16->32\\n            nn.BatchNorm2d(128),\\n            nn.GELU(),\\n            nn.ConvTranspose2d(128, 64, kernel_size=4, stride=2, padding=1),  # 32->64\\n            nn.BatchNorm2d(64),\\n            nn.GELU(),\\n            nn.Conv2d(64, num_classes, kernel_size=1),\\n        )\\n\\n    def forward(self, visual_features: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Args:\\n            visual_features: (B, N, D) fused features.\\n                N >= num_patches_per_image; first view is used.\\n        Returns:\\n            logits: (B, num_classes, out_resolution, out_resolution)\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if visual_features.shape[1] > self.num_patches_per_image:\\n            visual_features = visual_features[:, :self.num_patches_per_image, :]\\n\\n        B = visual_features.shape[0]\\n        x = self.project(visual_features)\\n        x = x.transpose(1, 2).reshape(B, -1, self.spatial_h, self.spatial_w)\\n        logits = self.decoder(x)\\n        return logits\\n\\n\\nclass FocalLoss(nn.Module):\\n    def __init__(self, alpha: float = 0.25, gamma: float = 2.0):\\n        super().__init__()\\n        self.alpha = alpha\\n        self.gamma = gamma\\n\\n    def forward(self, logits: torch.Tensor, targets: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Args:\\n            logits: (B, 1, H, W)\\n            targets: (B, H, W) binary\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        probs = torch.sigmoid(logits.squeeze(1))\\n        targets = targets.float()\\n        bce = F.binary_cross_entropy_with_logits(\\n            logits.squeeze(1), targets, reduction='none'\\n        )\\n        pt = torch.where(targets == 1, probs, 1 - probs)\\n        alpha_t = torch.where(targets == 1, self.alpha, 1 - self.alpha)\\n        focal_weight = alpha_t * (1 - pt) ** self.gamma\\n        return (focal_weight * bce).mean()\\n\\n\\nclass DepthHead(nn.Module):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Monocular depth prediction head, reusing BEVOccupancyHead architecture.\\n\\n    Input:  (B, N, D) patch features — first 256 tokens (16x16 spatial grid) are used.\\n    Output: (B, 1, out_resolution, out_resolution) continuous depth map (non-negative via ReLU).\\n\\n    Unlike BEVOccupancyHead which outputs binary logits, this head outputs\\n    continuous depth values with SmoothL1 regression loss.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, in_dim: int, hidden_dim: int = 256, out_resolution: int = 64,\\n                 num_patches_per_image: int = 256):\\n        super().__init__()\\n        self.num_patches_per_image = num_patches_per_image\\n        self.spatial_h = self.spatial_w = int(num_patches_per_image ** 0.5)  # 16\\n        self.out_resolution = out_resolution\\n\\n        self.project = nn.Linear(in_dim, hidden_dim)\\n        self.decoder = nn.Sequential(\\n            nn.ConvTranspose2d(hidden_dim, 128, kernel_size=4, stride=2, padding=1),  # 16->32\\n            nn.BatchNorm2d(128),\\n            nn.GELU(),\\n            nn.ConvTranspose2d(128, 64, kernel_size=4, stride=2, padding=1),  # 32->64\\n            nn.BatchNorm2d(64),\\n            nn.GELU(),\\n            nn.Conv2d(64, 1, kernel_size=1),\\n            nn.ReLU(),  # depth is non-negative\\n        )\\n\\n    def forward(self, visual_features: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Args:\\n            visual_features: (B, N, D) fused features.\\n                N >= num_patches_per_image; first view is used.\\n        Returns:\\n            depth: (B, 1, out_resolution, out_resolution) non-negative depth values.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if visual_features.shape[1] > self.num_patches_per_image:\\n            visual_features = visual_features[:, :self.num_patches_per_image, :]\\n\\n        B = visual_features.shape[0]\\n        x = self.project(visual_features)\\n        x = x.transpose(1, 2).reshape(B, -1, self.spatial_h, self.spatial_w)\\n        depth = self.decoder(x)\\n        return depth\\n\\n\\nclass DepthLoss(nn.Module):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"SmoothL1 loss for monocular depth prediction.\\n\\n    More robust to outliers than L1. Expects:\\n      pred:   (B, 1, H, W) predicted depth\\n      target: (B, H, W) ground-truth depth\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, beta: float = 1.0):\\n        super().__init__()\\n        self.loss_fn = nn.SmoothL1Loss(beta=beta)\\n\\n    def forward(self, pred: torch.Tensor, target: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:\\n        target = target.unsqueeze(1)  # (B, 1, H, W)\\n        return self.loss_fn(pred, target)\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The training completed successfully and the checkpoint was saved at:
`/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/occ_s1_fresh/openvla-7b+libero_spatial_no_noops+b8+lr-0.0005+lora-r32+dropout-0.0--image_aug--50000_chkpt`

And evaluation was run successfully from this checkpoint (the eval log exists). But the directory no longer exists - it was likely deleted to save space.

Since the Full OCC checkpoint doesn't exist, I need to use what IS available. The cutoff checkpoints DO exist and have occ_head weights. 

The cutoff_s1 checkpoint exists with occ_head weights, though it was trained with `occ_detach=True`, which means the weights differ from the Full OCC version. I could still use this for the gradient analysis and CKA since the analysis would measure how gradients flow through the encoder during backward passes - the key difference being that in Full OCC mode, the occ_loss gradients flow back through the encoder, whereas in the detached version they don't.

But here's the problem: if I run the gradient analysis on the cutoff checkpoint with `occ_detach=False`, I'm measuring gradient properties at weights that were never trained that way. The weights themselves learned under detached conditions, so analyzing them with full gradient flow doesn't reflect what the Full OCC model actually learned.

Let me reconsider what the paper is actually trying to demonstrate with the gradient analysis. off checkpoint with occ_detach=False would show what gradient flow looks like at those weights if we allowed full backprop through the OCC loss. It's not ideal since the weights come from constrained training, but it's still informative about the gradient landscape. I should tell the user the Full OCC checkpoint is missing, use cutoff_s1 as a substitute with this caveat clearly noted, and proceed with the analysis so the methodology is ready when the correct checkpoint becomes available. off_s1 checkpoint was trained with occ_detach=True. But when we load it for gradient analysis, we can set occ_detach=False and compute gradients. This means:
- The weights reflect cutoff training (no OCC gradient influence on encoder)
- But the gradient computation shows what gradients WOULD flow through

For the CKA feature extraction, we're just looking at features, which depend only on weights, not on gradient settings. So the CKA features from cutoff_s1 would be "features from a model trained without OCC gradient flow in encoder".

For the Full OCC condition, we'd want to show "features from a model trained WITH OCC gradient flow" - which would be different because the training shaped those features.

OK, I'll inform the user that the Full OCC checkpoint is missing and proceed with cutoff_s1 as a proxy, clearly documenting this. The scripts and methodology will be transferable.

Let me check if there's another server where these checkpoints might be stored, and I should also look at the project config to see what's configured. I'll use cutoff_s1 as a fallback and document that clearly, then dive into the finetune.py file to understand how the forward pass works.

```text
Now I can see the key OCC logic. Let me understand the architecture:

1. The model uses `OpenVLAForActionPrediction` from `prismatic.extern.hf.modeling_prismatic`
2. It has a `_cached_patch_features` attribute on the base model that stores visual patch features
3. The OCC head takes these patch features and predicts BEV occupancy
4. When `occ_detach=True`, the patch_features are detached before being fed to OCC head
5. OCC loss is computed with FocalLoss
6. The total loss = action_loss + occ_loss_weight * occ_loss


7. The vision backbone is a dual encoder (DINOv2 + SigLIP) that's fused together, and the OCC head takes the first 256 tokens (16x16) from the patch features

I need to trace through the modeling code to see exactly where these patch features are cached and what dimensions they have at each layer.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I understand the architecture:

1. **Vision backbone**: DINOv2 + SigLIP (fused) → `patch_features` shape `(B, 256, D_dino + D_siglip)`
   - `featurizer` = SigLIP
   - `fused_featurizer` = DINOv2
   - They process different channel splits of `pixel_values` (which has 6 channels: 3 for SigLIP + 3 for DINOv2)
   - Features are concatenated along dim=2
2. `_cached_patch_features` = raw fused features before projection


3. The projector transforms the fused vision features into the LLM's embedding space, then the LLM processes those projected features alongside text tokens
4. Two task heads branch off: the action head operates on LLM hidden states while the occupancy head uses the raw cached patch features before projection

For gradient analysis, I need to instrument the SigLIP and DINOv2 backbone layers, the projector, and potentially some LLM layers to track how gradients flow through the system. For CKA analysis, I'll need to hook into comparable layers across the different components.

Now I should check how the occupancy data is actually loaded to understand the batch structure and what I'm working with.
```

### Assistant

_source line 154_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a clear picture:

**Model Architecture:**
- Vision backbone: DINOv2 (`vit_large_patch14_reg4_dinov2.lvd142m`) + SigLIP (`vit_so400m_patch14_siglip_224`)
  - DINOv2 ViT-L: embed_dim=1024, 24 blocks
  - SigLIP ViT-SO400M: embed_dim=1152, 27 blocks
  - Fused embed_dim = 1024 + 1152 = 2176


- Images are processed at 224x224 resolution with 14-pixel patches, creating a 16x16 grid of 256 patches per image, then fed through a projector MLP that expands and compresses the fused embeddings down to 4096 dimensions to match the Llama-2-7B LLM, while separate heads handle action regression and occupancy prediction at 64x64 resolution.

Now I see the checkpoint structure: the model weights are stored across multiple safetensors shards with LoRA already merged into the base weights, plus separate checkpoints for the action head, occupancy head, and proprioceptive projector. Since the LoRA is pre-merged, I can load everything directly without needing PEFT. I'm ready to write the gradient analysis script, which will load the merged model and all component heads from their checkpoints, then set up the data pipeline.

For each batch, I'll run a forward pass to get the action logits and cached patch features, compute the action loss and backpropagate to capture encoder gradients, then feed those cached features to the occupancy head to compute its loss and backpropagate again. The key insight is that the action loss flows backward through the LLM and projector into the vision backbone, while the occupancy loss flows directly from the occupancy head through the cached patch features—so I need to carefully track whether those features are detached to understand the gradient flow.

The tricky part is that both losses share the vision backbone in the computational graph, so when I backpropagate the action loss, I need to use `retain_graph=True` to keep the graph intact for the occupancy loss backward pass. Without it, the graph gets freed and the second backward call will fail. After recording gradients from each backward pass, I'll zero out the gradients before the next iteration.

For capturing layer-wise gradients, I'm planning to use backward hooks on the ViT blocks to intercept and record the gradients flowing through each layer.

I'll also need forward hooks to capture intermediate activations for CKA analysis. Now I'm thinking through how to load the checkpoint properly—the model itself can be loaded directly with `from_pretrained()` since the LoRA weights were merged during training, but the action and occupancy heads are stored as separate `.pt` files that need to be loaded with `torch.load()`.
```

dimensionality of the positional encoding\\n\\n    def forward(self, x):\\n        # x: (batch_size,)\\n        device = x.device\\n        assert self.dim % 2 == 0, f\\\"# dimensions must be even but got {self.dim}\\\"\\n        half_dim = self.dim // 2\\n        exponent = torch.arange(half_dim, device=device) * -math.log(10000) / (half_dim - 1)  # (batch_size, chunk_len, action_dim * hidden_dim)\\n        # Get diffusion model's noise prediction.\\n        noise_pred = self.noise_predictor(rearranged_actions_hidden_states)\\n        return noise_pred\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

OK, so from the collator, `labels` is padded to the same length as `input_ids`, which is the TEXT token sequence (does NOT include vision patch positions). The labels only contain text tokens, with IGNORE_INDEX for non-action positions.

So:
- `input_ids` shape: (B, text_seq_len)
- `labels` shape: (B, text_seq_len) 
- `hidden_states[-1]` shape: (B, num_patches + text_seq_len, D) — vision patches are prepended during forward

Now:
- `text_hidden_states = hidden_states[-1][:, num_patches:-1]` → (B, text_seq_len - 1, D)


- `mask = get_current_action_mask(labels)` → (B, text_seq_len)

There's a shape mismatch here because in causal language modeling, the hidden state at position i predicts the token at position i+1. So I need to align them by dropping the last hidden state (which has nothing to predict) and the first label (which has nothing predicting it).

The code shifts the hidden states and labels accordingly, then applies the action mask to the shifted labels to get the right dimensions for masking the predictions.

But there's a dimension mismatch here—text_hidden_states is (B, text_seq_len - 1, D) while the mask is (B, text_seq_len), so applying the mask directly should fail. Unless the last token is always padding with IGNORE_INDEX, making the last mask value False, which would mean mask[:, :-1] and mask produce equivalent results when used as a boolean index. Though PyTorch would still check dimensions first.

But that slice `[:, num_patches:-1]` is giving me one fewer token than the labels, which expects the full text sequence length—this is the core mismatch I keep running into.
